######################### AlexNet (Conv. DNN) ############################

two tests planned:
1-2% accuracy drop test on three seeds. Similar budget like MNIST tests, 
Neuron pruning phase budget to be tested with 80% of Accuracy budget.
	Note on results below: test did not consume full accuracy budget as it cannot find more candidate neurons to prune (both branches). Proceeded with 2nd test. Same test with different seed is TBD.
2-Matched-count study: 20% and 30% on three seeds (then continue up to 2% accuracy budget as usual).
	Note on results below: A (iterative Comm Cost method) relative to D (Taylor) is consistently better for all 3-seeds. 
						   FLOPs and size are a tie, and A used slightly less accuracy (−1.77 against −1.82 pp). 
						   So A is also not buying its lower cost with extra accuracy..
JUMP TO LINE 760 FOR THE SUMMARY OF THE RESULTS

SUMMARY OF THE RESULTS
Final state, n30, branch A (IterCC) against D (Taylor)
						seed 42			seed 10042		seed 20042		3-seed mean
remaining WCTG, A		10,458			10,023			9,868			−97.57% (2.43% left)
remaining WCTG, D		11,451			10,226			11,172			−97.36% (2.64% left)
A relative to D			−8.7%			−2.0%			−11.7%			about −7.5%
final accuracy, A / D	−1.83 / −1.82	−1.72 / −1.87	−1.76 / −1.78	−1.77 / −1.82
A looks better on the other final columns. Cross-group cost is −79.4% against −78.0%, and edges are −98.30% against −97.99%. 
FLOPs and size are a tie, and A used slightly less accuracy (−1.77 against −1.82 pp). So A is not buying its lower cost with extra accuracy.
Significance: the paired difference is −833 ± 328 (standard error), which gives p of about 0.13 with 3 seeds, by my own quick calculation. 
Three of three seeds in the same direction is consistent, but it is not enough to claim it. 
Earlier n20 gave A lower in 2 of 3 seeds, so it is 5 of 6 over both fractions.
Where the advantage comes from. At the neuron end Taylor leads on WCTG by about 2.4 points and on FLOPs by 7, and IterCC leads cross-group cost by about 26 points (−19.5% against +6.3%). 
The edge phase narrows these gaps, and IterCC ends slightly ahead. I’d say IterCC enters the edge phase with a better cross-group structure, but I haven’t tested that as a mechanism, so word it as a hypothesis.

TBD: Adding two seeds (30042, 40042) gives n=5 and may firm the AlexNet claim up. A seed takes about 70–95 minutes for A and D together.

For the paper, a 3-seed result with 3 of 3 in the same direction and a 7–8% lower remaining cost is honestly described as “comparable, with a small consistent edge for IterCC”. 
I’d wait for n=5 before calling it an advantage.

CA="MODEL_PATH=DNN_AlexNet_CIFAR10_full200.pth FT_LR=1e-4 L1_TARGET=0.10 BRANCHES=B,D,G L2_LONELY=0 NEURON_SEQUENTIAL=1 NEURON_STEP_FRAC=0.015 SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL=0.005 EVAL_FAST=1 GRAPH_FAST=1 EDGE_FAST=1 BRIDGE_GROUPS_FAST=1"
for s in 42 10042 20042; do
  env $CA NEURON_MAX_FRAC=0.30 NEURON_TOL=0.05 SNAPSHOT_NEURON_END=1 RUN_TAG=_n30ne_L2off SEED=$s python -u main_AlexNet_CIFAR10_e2e_AccuracyBudget_Metaheuristic_v8.py > output_AlexNet_v8_n30ne_L2off_seed$s.log 2>&1 && echo "done AlexNet n30ne L2off seed $s $(date)" >> overnight_progress2.txt || echo "FAILED AlexNet n30ne L2off seed $s $(date)" >> overnight_progress2.txt
done
for s in 42 10042 20042; do
  SEED=$s L2_LONELY=0 EVAL_FAST=1 GRAPH_FAST=1 BRIDGE_FAST=1 python -u main_Autoenc_3072_128_CIFAR10_e2e_AccuracyBudget_Metaheuristic_v3.py > output_Autoenc_3072_128_CIFAR10_v3_L2off_seed$s.log 2>&1 && echo "done AE128 L2off seed $s $(date)" >> overnight_progress2.txt || echo "FAILED AE128 L2off seed $s $(date)" >> overnight_progress2.txt
done
' > /dev/null 2>&1 &

L2_lonely = OFF; branch B & D (plain-L2 -> L2_lonely = 0) Reading
IterCC against Taylor is a tie to a small edge. B beats D by +1.08 points on average, but only because of seed 10042, where D’s edge phase stopped early (-95.77, edges only -93 percent). The per-seed differences are +0.14, +3.12 and -0.02. Without that seed it is a tie. B against G is +1.3 on every seed (+1.34, +1.42, +1.26). That is a consistent sign, though G is a different edge method.
Plain L2 reaches a lower remaining WCTG than Bridge-Link at equal accuracy, again. Remaining WCTG: B 1.13 percent, D 2.21 and G 2.47. Plain L2 beat Bridge-Link on 5 of the 6 new per-seed comparisons (B against G on all three, D against G on two). With the earlier 11 of 12, that makes 16 of 18. The pattern holds on AlexNet at all three settings.
Dead-neuron removal decides FLOPs. Plain B and D keep all 2,240 nodes after the neuron phase. Bridge-Link (G) removes about 960 more, ending at 1,281 nodes and FLOPs -98.7 percent, against -38 to -45 percent for plain L2. That is the same trade-off as on LeNet5, only much larger.
Neuron-phase end, as before. IterCC’s cross-group cost is -19.5 percent against +6.3 for Taylor (about 26 points better), while Taylor leads WCTG at the neuron end (-54.6 against -52.0), FLOPs (-45.0 against -37.9) and size.
